SAFETY NOTICE

   This unit generates a high voltage which is dangerous to life. The operator
must be careful and observe every safety regulation at all times. Keep clear

of all live parts. Never make connection while the unit is in operation. Severe

and possibly fatal shocks may be experienced, especially if the power unit is

operating on wet or damp ground. Adequate ventilation must be provided, if

the power unit is operated in a confined space. Exhaust gases produced are
poisonous, and excessive inhalations may result in severe sickness or death.
Do not service power unit with gasoline while it is running or if a radio trans-
mitter is operating in close proximity to the power unit. Avoid spilling
gasoline on a hot engine.

   Caution: ALWAYS MAINTAIN THE PROPER OIL LEVELS IN THE
CRANKCASE AND IN THE AIR CLEANER OF THE ENGINE, AND
LUBRICATE THE ENGINE AT PROPER INTERVALS AS RECOM-
MENDED IN THIS INSTRUCTION MANUAL.

Guards and shields: All moving or operating parts have been adequately

guarded or shielded to provide maximum protection. Do not remove any

guards, shields, screens, etc., to perform service or maintenance work while

the power unit is in operation.  ‘To do so, removes the safety features provided
for your protection.

   Caution: TOOLS, OILCAN, BOLTS, AND OTHER LOOSE OBJECTS
MUST NOT BE PERMITTED TO LIE ON, OR NEAR THE GENERATOR
SET WHERE THEY MIGHT FALL INTO THE GENERATOR OR BE
DRAWN INTO THE ROTATING PARTS BY MAGNETIC ATTRAC-
TION, CAUSING SEVERE DAMAGE TO THE WINDING.

c. Ducr Connections. The radiator is equip-

ped with a metal frame to which a duct of canvas,

or other material, may be attached to direct the

hot air from the engine to the atmosphere. If a

duct is used for this purpose, provision must be

made for the entry of air into the engine room to

replace the air which is forced out through the

duct by the fan. If a metal duct is used, install

a short section of flexible material, such as canvas,

between the frame on the radiator and the metal

duct. The frame is drilled to facilitate the instal-

lation of such equipment. (Duct material is not

provided as part of the unit.)

22. Overload Protection

   The air circuit breaker on the switchboard is
equipped with a thermal overload device, a mag-
netic short circuit trip, a shunt trip, and an auxil-
iary switch in series with the shunt trip. The
overload thermal device is a metallic strip which
changes shape with temperature changes. This
causes the circuit breaker mechanism to operate
and open the circuits when currents are higher
than normal. The device also acts as an inverse

                                                                        @. SEVERE OprERATING ConpiTions. Under
                                                                     severe operating conditions, or over prolonged
                                                                     periods of operation in surrounding high tempera-
                                                                     tures, be sure to reduce the time intervals between
                                                                     lubrication periods for the unit. Under desert
                                                                     conditions take precautions to prevent sand and
                                                                     dust from blowing around the unit, either during
                                                                     operation or when idle. Be careful to keep dirt,
                                                                     sand, and other foreign matter from filtering into
                                                                     lubricated parts. Cover the equipment when not
                                                                     in use. Thorough cleanliness is imperative.
                                                                     Instead of adding new lubricants at regular
                                                                     intervals, clean and lubricate all moving parts
                                                                     whenever practicable. If possible, inspect and
                                                                     clean the equipment daily. Inspect the air filters
                                                                     and similar protective devices every day and clean
                                                                     them whenever necessary. Refer to TB SIG 69
                                                                     (Lubrication of Ground Signal Equipment).

Tech.   Semi-                                         Instructions
insp.  snare Monthly

                                                      BEFORE OPERATION SERVICE

       if  1          Perform the before-operation services (sec. IV) to determine if the power unit is in a satisfactory

                      condition to make the test safely, and that it is adequately supplied with fuel, oil, and coolant.

       2   2          Lubrication. Check the power unit to determine if lubrication is needed. Consult the main-

                      tenance roster, or other lubrication records, for the date on which lubrication was last performed.

                      Check these dates against the lubrication instructions, and apply lubricants where necessary.

                      Make a record of all completed jobs to avoid the possibility of overlubrication by uninformed

                      maintenance personnel and plant operators. On any unit where disassembly was necessary for

                      inspection purposes, lubrication must be performed, unless the unit is scheduled for repair. Lubri-

                      cate in the following manner: Lubricate all points of the power unit in accordance with instruction

                      in section II, chapter 3, current lubrication bulletins, or directives. Use only clean lubricant.

                      Keep all lubricant containers and dispensers covered, except when withdrawing lubricant. Before

                      applying lubricant, clean the lubrication fitting or plug, so that dirt will not enter with the lubri-

                      cant. If lubrication fittings, flexible lines, vents, or plugs are missing or damaged, replace them

                      immediately. Clean the hole in which the new fitting is to be installed, install the fitting, and

                      lubricate the unit. On all unsealed bushings or joints, apply the lubricant until it appears at the

                      openings. On units such as universal joints, which are provided with lubricant retainer seals,

                      use an appropriate hand-operated grease gun, and do not force the lubricant beyond the seals.

                      Open any clogged lubrication passages until lubricant is properly delivered. When draining oil

                      from the engine, do so immediately after the engine has been operating, and is at operating tem-

                      perature. Refill to the correct level with specified oil as soon as draining is completed.

                      Caution: Do not fill to overflowing. Reinstall all drain and filler plugs securely. Make cer-

                      tain all gaskets are in good condition and in place on the reinstalled plugs. Do not apply more than

                      the specified amount of lubricant. Wipe off any excess lubricant that may drip onto rubber parts

                      or the body of the power unit.

       3   3          Tools and Equipment. Check all the standard tools and equipment against the packing list to

                      be sure they are all present. Inspect to be sure that tools are in good condition, clean, and prop-

                      erly stowed or securely mounted. Examine tools which have cutting edges to see that they are

                      sharp.

       4   4          Fire Extinguisher. Examine the fire extinguisher; be sure that it is in good condition, and that

                      it is filled and ready for operation. Clean dirt and corrosion from the nozzle.

       5   5          Publications. See that the following are present in a legible condition:

                      TM 11-998.

                      WD AGO Form 48 (Trip Ticket).

                      WD AGO Form 464 (Work Sheet for Engineer Equipment).

                      WD AGO Form 460 (Preventive Maintenance Roster).

                      WD AGO Form 468 (Unsatisfactory Equipment Report).

       6   6          Appearance. Examine the unit for cleanliness and be sure the paint is in good condition. Pay

                      particular attention to any bright spots in finish that might cause glare or reflection. Inspect

                      markings and identification (including identification plates and mountings) for legibility.

                                                                                                                   TM 998-15
                                                                        Figure 25. Cylinder head bolt tightening sequence.

50

Tech. Semi |Moathly                                        Instructions

insp. annua

                                         ENGINE AND ACCESSORIES

11  11  11           Cylinder Heads, Manifold and Gasket. Check for cracks, or indications of oil, water, or compres-

                     sion leaks around studs, cap screws, and gaskets.

                     Caution: Ordinarily, cvlinder heads should not be tightened unless there is a definite indication

                     of looseness or leaks. If tightening is necessary, use a torque-indicating wrench and tighten in

                     the proper sequence (fig. 25). Torque wrench tension should be 3,500 to 3,600 inch-pounds.

                     Tighten the hold-down studs in two or three successive stages, starting with the center, and

                     working toward each and alternately. This insures even pressure over the entire surface of the

                     cylinder head and gasket. Check the intake and exhaust manifolds. Be sure that they are in

                     good condition and secure, and that gaskets are in good condition ana not leaking. Tighten all

                     manifold assembly, mounting, and exhaust pipe nuts evenly and securely.

12  12  12           Valve Mechanism. To test valves, crank the engine. The valves should seat fully and should

                     hold a charge so that the hand crank wil! bounce back, or rock as the engine is turned over. If the

                     engine does not offer added resistance to the crank on the compression stroke, leakage can generally

                     be heard as a hiss. If it comes most distinctly through the intake, the intake valve is leaking;

                     if most distinctly through the exhaust, the exhaust valve is leaking; if through the breather, the

                     leak is past the pistons and rings. Leaks at the spark plugs and injector nozzles can be seen while

                     the engine is running. Leaking valves should be inspected and reground (par. 99).

12                   Remove the valve springs and check the valve stems and the fits in the guides. If wear is

                     excessive, replace the valve and the guide. Otherwise it will be impossible to secure a tight seat

                     by grinding. Notice particularly the exhaust valve guides, and valve stem shoulders. The

                     valve guide should not project into valve gas passage, and the shoulders on the stems should be

                     sharp. ‘The shoulders should be %2 to %e inch below the top of the valve guide when the valve

                     is seated. Replace any worn valve guides as directed in paragraph 101.

12  12  12           Examine valve tappet clearances while the engine is hot. Valve tappets, rocker arms, shafts,

                     and springs should appear in good condition, correctly assembled, and secure. Oil should be

                     delivered properly. Make sure valve cover gaskets are in good condition. Test the oil lead to

                     the rocker arm shaft to make sure it is not clogged.

        12           Adjust. Release the locknut and adjust the tappet clearances, with the engine cold, to

                     0.025 to 0.027 inch (intake) and 0.030 to 0.032 inch (exhaust) running clearance. If valves

                     have been ground, add 0.002 inch to these figures for the first 10 hours of operation. Tighten

                     the locknut after clearances have been adjusted.

14  14  14           Crankcase Breathers. With engine idling, examine crankcase, valve covers, and timing gear

                     cover for oil leaks. Stop the engine, and, after the oil has drained into the crankcase, see if the

                     oil is at the proper level. Through one of the inspection openings, see if the crankcase oil has

                     formed sludge and coated the interior of the engine or whipped up into a thick froth. If it has,

                     oil changes should be more frequent.

                       Note. Ifan oil change is due, drain the crankcase and refill to the proper level with specified oil.  Do not start the engine again
                     until item 15 is completed.

15  15  15           Oil Filters. Inspect the primary lubricating oil filter, the continuous lubricating oil purifier, and

                     all external engine lubricating oil lines, to see that they are in good condition, secure, and do

                     not leak. If due, or if condition indicates such action is desired, replace filter elements.

15  15  15           Inspect the oil cooler element for sludge and gum deposits. Clean the oil cooler (par. 75),

                     if such a treatment is due or if inspection reveals excessive dirt and sludge.

16  16  16           Radiator. See that the core, shell, mounting, hose, connections, caps, and gaskets are in good

                     condition, securely mounted and connected, correctly assembled, and do not leak. See that the

                     core air passages are not obstructed with dirt, insects, or trash, and that the core fins are not

                     badly bent. Examine the coolant to see if it is so contaminated with rust, oil, or other foreign

                     matter that the cooling system should be drained. If cleaning is necessary, proceed as follows:

                     Drain the radiator by opening the drain cocks on either side of the engine, at the bottom of each

                     series of cylinder blocks, on either side of the radiator, at the water pump inlet, and at the bottom

                     of the oil cooler.

                     If ethylene-glycol antifreeze is used, save the drainings for reuse. Clean the cooling system

                     according to current directives, using only specified cleaner. Flush cleaner from the entire cooling

                     system with clean water. Refill radiator with coolant, adding specified inhibitor, unless new

                                                                                                                             51

             Semi-  Monthly                                             Instructions
            annual

                                                      ENGINE AND ACCESSORIES—Continued

    ca

                             antifreeze is used, which contains inhibitor. Do not fill to top; allow room for expansion. If
                             antifreeze is used, determine its protective value and make a record in the Space provided on the
                             reverse side of the work sheet. Clean the dirt, insects, and trash from the core by blowing out
                             with compressed air, or with a stream of water.

                                Caution. Do not clean the core with steam.  ‘Tighten all loose radiator mountings and hose
                             clamps.

        hs      Ws  Ly       Water Pump, Fan, Shroud. Check the water pump to see that it is in good condition, is not

                             leaking, and is securely installed. Remove the intake fitting and make sure that the pump rotor
                             is tight on the shaft. Carefully tighten packing gland nut on water pump. Do not overtighten
                             as this will cause scoring of the shaft and leakage. Loosen fan-drive belts, and keep them loose
                             until adjustment is made. Examine fan shaft for end play and loose bearings. Inspect the fan
                             blades to see that they are in good condition and properly secured to the hub. Be sure that the
                             fan guard is in good condition and securely mounted.

    18      18      18       Belts and Pulleys. Check the three fan belts for evidence of fraying, excessive wear, and dete-

                             rioration. Be sure that all pulleys and hubs are in good condition, securely mounted, and correctly
                             alined.

               18   18                Adjust. Loosen the nut holding the fan belt guide in position, and slide the guide up until

    19| ibe                  the fan belts can be depressed about % inch. Then tighten nut.
                    19       Oil Pump, Pressure Relief Valve. Check the reading on the oil pressure gage when the engine

                             is hot. If the pressure reads 40 pounds per square inch, the oil pump is operating properly, the
                             automatic oil pressure valve is correctly adjusted, and the entire lubrication system is operating
                             satisfactorily. Pressure below 40 pounds per square inch may be caused by improper adjustment
                             of the pressure valve, dirt in the pressure valve, a clogged intake screen, defects in the oil pump,
                             or a ruptured oil line. Before changing the adjustment on the valve, make sure the crankease oil
                             is in good condition, clean the oil pump intake screen and the oil relief valve. Check the unit for
                             ruptured oil lines. To adjust the valve, proceed as follows: With engine at operating speed, and
                             running at the governed speed, remove the cap (FH, fig. 26), and with a screw driver inserted in the
                             slot of the pressure-adjusting screw (C, fig. 26), turn the screw to raise or lower the cil pressure.
                             If the oil pump is defective, replace it.

   29      30 30
   30                    Cylinder Head, Manifold and Gasket. Check for cracks, or indications of oil, water, or com-
   31      31 31
   32      32 32         pression leaks around studs, cap screws, and gasket.
                            Caution: Cylinder heads should not be tightened unless there is evidence of looseness or leaks.
    33      33 33
    35      35 35        If tightening is necessary, use a speed wrench and draw the fastenings fairly tight. Start with the

    36      36 36        center studs, and work toward the ends, alternately.                               and in good con-
            36 36           Examine the combination intake and exhaust manifold; be sure it is secure        Securely tighten
    37
                     36  dition. See that all gaskets are in good condition; be sure they are not leaking.
            atel eae
            37 37        all manifold assembly, mounting, and exhaust pipe nuts.
                            Crankcase Breathers. With engine idling, examine crankcase and valve covers for oil leaks.

                         Stop the engine, and, after oil has drained into the crankcase, see if the oil is at the proper level.
                            Valves. Crank the engine and listen for a hissing noise at the air intake and exhaust outlet.

                         If such noise is heard, valves are leaking and must be reground. Inspect the valve tappet clear-
                         ances. Tappet clearance dimensions, with engine oil at room temperature, are 0.005 to 0.007
                         inch intake, and 0.007 to 0.009 inch exhaust. Add 0.002 inch to the dimensions given for the first

                         10 hours of operation after a regrind job.
                            Ignition System. All high-voltage wiring (spark plug cables) should be in good condition and

                         securely fastened to all support mountings and terminals. See that all insulation and connections
                         are clean. Check the spark plug and magneto as described in paragraphs 84 and 86.

                            Fuel Pump, Carburetor, and Governor. See that the carburetor, choke, governor control linkage,
                         throttle, and governor are in good condition, correctly assembled, and securely installed; that the
                         carburetor does not leak; that the control linkage is not excessively worn; that the choke valve
                         opens fully when it is operated; and that the governor is secure and properly sealed. Inspect the
                         gasoline strainer for dirt. When dirt begins to collect in the bottom of the glass bowl, close the
                         shut-off cock, unscrew the thumbscrew retainer, and remove and clean the glass bowl. When
                         replacing, be sure that the bowl seats properly, and that the gasket has not been damaged.

                             Clutch and Transmission. Start the cranking engine and engage the clutch. The clutch should
                         just begin to engage when the handle has moved about one third of the total travel. If it does
                         not begin to engage at this position, adjust the clutch as described in paragraph 103d.

                             Magneto or Distributor. See that the magneto is clean and dry. If the unit is operated under
                         severe conditions, remove the distributor spool, wipe it off, and see that the carbon brushes work
                         freely and have a slight spring tension. Check the contact points and resurface with a fine stone
                         if there is evidence of pitting. Adjust to a maximum opening of 0.014 to 0.016 inch. Lubricate
                         at the same time, applying 3 or 4 drops of OE 50 oil tothe cam wick and 1 drop to the breaker-bar
                         bearing. Do not get oil on the breaker points or allow oil to flow over the interior of the magneto.

                             Spark Plugs. Remove and examine the spark plugs. Make sure that their insulators are clean
                         and in good condition. Note any evidence of leakage around the insulators or gaskets.

                             Examine the spark plugs for poor condition, paying particular attention to broken insulators,
                         excessive carbon deposits, and to electrodes which are burned thin. Replace unserviceable plugs.
                          Check the spark plug gap with a feeler gage. Gap should be from 0.025 to 0.030 inch. Report
                         excessive deposits or damaged insulators, as these conditions may indicate incorrect heat range.

                                     Clean. Clean the deposits from the electrodes with kerosene, or fuel oil, and a stiff brush.

                                Check the insulators to see if they are cracked.
                                   Adjust. Adjust gaps to 0.025 to 0.030 inch. When reinstalling plugs, use new gaskets and

                               be sure not to overtighten them as this may cause distortion and damage.
                            Oil Pump and Relief Valve. Inspect the oil pump screen for cleanliness. If the need for clean-
                         ing is indicated by the condition of the screen, remove the oil pan and unscrew the screen assembly
                         from the pump. Put the screen in solvent (SD) and allow it to remain long enough to soften
                         any carbon which may have accumulated. Scrape off carbon with a putty knife. Be careful not
                         to puncture or damage the screen. Observe the reading of the cranking engine oil pressure gage.
                         Hot oil pressure, with engine at operating speed, should be 15 pounds. If pressure is low, and oil
                         supply is adequate, inspect the pressure relief valve for chips or dirt, and the oil pump for a sheared
                         drive pin or key. If the pressure valve requires cleaning, remove the protecting cap, the adjust-
                         ing screw lock, adjusting screw, pressure contact spring, and the cylindrical valve plunger. Wash’
                         all parts in solvent (SD) including the valve seat in the crankcase. Scrape away any hardened
                         carbon. Then reassemble. If, after the oil screen has been cleaned, the pressure valve is checked
                         and cleaned, and the supply of lubricating oil in the crankcase is sufficient, remove the oil pan and
                         take off the back plate of the oil pump. Then crank the engine over. If the pump rotor with the
                         two sliding vanes does not rotate as the engine turns, the pump is defective and must be repaired.

                         STARTING OR AUXILIARY ENGINE—Continued

    38  38      38       Fuel Pumps and Housing. Service as follows:

                            Fuel supply pump. See that the fuel supply pump and lines are in good condition, are secure,
                         and are not leaking. Replace any pump that does not produce proper pressure. If there is
                         evidence of oil leaking around the drive shaft, and if drawing up on the packing nut (on the right
                         side of the pump) does not stop the leak, back off the packing nut, remove the old packing, and
                         replace with new packing, using as much as possible. Do not overpack or packing nut will not
                         start. Tighten the nut sufficiently to stop any oil leakage.

                            Fuel injection pump. See that     all high-pressure fuel fittings are kept tight and do not leak.
                         Inspect the coupling adjustment     of the gear-driven fuel injection pump. If the coupling has
                         slipped, or if any floating member  of the coupling shows signs of wear, timing of the fuel injection
                         pump will be seriously affected.    If the fuel injection pump does not operate properly, it should
                         be replaced as a unit.

                            Adjust. Adjust the timing of the coupling and the fuel pump in the following manner:
                         Rotate the engine until the No. 1 piston is coming up on compression. Continue to crank
                         the engine until the timing pin registers with the FP mark on the flywheel. Do not move the
                         engine from this position during the remainder of the timing adjustments, which are made
                         by moving the pump itself. Rotate the pump and its half of the coupling in the same direc-
                         tion as its driving shaft moves, and bring the timing mark on the coupling hub in line with
                         the indicator point on the end of the pump housing. With the pump in this position, there
                         will be two of the multiple holes in the flange of the driving member of the coupling in aline-
                         ment with the driven member attached to the pump shaft. Insert the cap screws in these
                         holes and draw them snugly and evenly into place.

                            Caution: Never break any fuel injection pump connections, or remove the pump without
                         first cleaning with fuel oil. As soon as connections are broken, cover the openings carefully
                         with clean cloths tied in place,

    40  40      40)      Filters. Examine the primary filter, the clarifier, fuel lines, and connections. Be sure that

                         | they are secure, in good condition, and are not leaking. If due, or if inspection reveals cleaning
                         or replacement of the filter elements is desired, replace the filter element in the clarifier as directed
                         in paragraph 40k, and clean the primary filter as instructed in paragraph 401.
    4]  4]      4]       Air Cleaners. Inspect the connections to both air cleaners. If they show signs of deterioration,

                         replace them, To eliminate strain on connections, be sure the pipes line up. All joints between
                         the air cleaner and the manifold and cylinders must be tight to secure efficient operation of the
                         unit. All gaskets should be in good condition, and the bolts drawn up tight. Clean and service
                         as instructed in paragraph 93.
    42
        42      42       Nozzles and Injectors. Inspect the fuel injection nozzles for spray pattern and opening pressure .

                         To observe the pattern, remove one nozzle at a time and connect it to the fuel line. Run the
                         engine on the remaining cylinders so that the injector being tested sprays into the atmosphere.
                         Check the spray against those shown in figure 27.

                         Caution: Keep hands and other parts of the body out of the path of the spray when testing.
                         The fuel is ejected with sufficient force to penetrate the flesh.
                         (A) shows the correct pattern; (B) indicates that the balls of the seats of the check valve are
                         worn, the spring is deformed, or dirt is in the valves; (C) indicates the sprays run together, prob-
                         ably due to worn nozzle holes. In (D) dirt in the nozzle holes or canals has caused an uneven
                         spray. If the spray openings of the injectors are clogged, clean them by inserting a thin, hard,
                         brass wire, not larger than #28, or 0.0126 inch (0.317 mm). If the spray pattern does not then
                         conform to the correct pattern (A), the injectors and nozzles must be cleaned (par. 91). If clean-
                         ing does not correct the trouble, replace the worn parts, or the entire assembly.
                         Inspect the primer spray nozzle. Be sure that it is not clogged or dirty. Disassemble the
                         nozzle and insert a wire through the openings.
    43  43      43       Tank, Filler Element, Cap and Gasket. Inspect the fuel oil and gasoline tanks to see that they

                         are in good condition and securely mounted. Examine caps for defective gaskets or plugged vents.
                         See that the filler necks are in good condition, and the caps fit securely. Check fuel lines and
                         fittings. Make sure that they are in good condition, supported securely, and are not leaking.

       49               46       Spark Plugs. Check the spark plugs. Make sure that their insulators are clean and in good

56                               condition. Note any evidence of leakage around the insulators, or gaskets.

                46      46       Remove the spark plugs, one at a time, and check for condition. Clean and replace each plug

                                 before removing those in the other cylinders. Pay particular attention to broken insulators,
                                 excessive carbon deposits, and electrodes which are burned thin. Report excessive deposits or

                                 damaged insulators, as these conditions may indicate incorrect heat range.

                                     Clean. After the plugs have been removed, wash them in solvent (SD) and dry. Check
                                 the insulators at this time to determine if they are cracked, or have lost their glaze due to
                                 harsh cleaning methods (sand blasting, filing, seraping, or grinding). If any of the plugs are
                                 defective, replace them with the same kind of plugs as used in the original equipment. Do
                                 not replace cleaned plugs until excess fuel has been purged from the cylinders. To purge, shut off
                                 the fuel oil by holding the stop lever in OFF position. With one spark plug removed, start
                                 and operate the unit on gasoline in the same manner as for operation in arctic climates (par.
                                 23). Run the engine on five cylinders at a moderate speed. This will blow the accumulated
                                 fuel out of the combustion chamber through the spark plug opening. After operating for 30
                                 seconds in this manner, stop priming the engine with gasoline. Shut the unit down, and replace

                                 the dried spark plug. Repeat these operations for each cylinder.

                                 Caution: The spark plugs used in the oil engine have electrodes that project much
                                 further into the combustion chamber, and are much sturdier than conventional plugs
                                 used in ordinary gasoline engines. Ordinary gasoline engine plugs will not fire the charge in this
                                 engine, and cannot be used. Always replace plugs with new ones identical to those removed.
                        46       Adjust. Adjust the gaps to 0.018 to 0.020 inch (0.467 to 0.483 mm) with a feeler gage.

                47      47       Battery. Inspect battery case for cracks and leaks. Clean top of battery. Inspect cables,

                                 terminals, bolts, posts, straps, and hold-downs for good condition. Test specific gravity and make
                                 record on WD AGO Form 464. Specific gravity readings below 1.260 indicate battery should be
                                 recharged. Electrolyte level should be one-half inch above the tops of the plates. Bring elec- .
                                 trolyte to proper level by adding distilled water. Clean entire battery and carrier. Repaint
                                 carrier if corroded. Clean battery cable terminals, terminal bolts and nuts, and battery posts,
                                 and grease them lightly. Inspect bolts for serviceability. Carefully tighten terminals and hold-
                                 downs to avoid damage to battery.

                48      48       Battery-Charging Generator, Voltage Regulator, and Ammeter. Note if these items are in good

                                 condition, securely mounted, and if wiring connections are clean and secure. During operation of
                                 the unit, the ammeter should show a positive reading. If it shows a negative reading, batteries
                                 may be improperly connected, battery-charging generator may require servicing, or voltage regu-
                                 lator may be defective. Check battery connections (par. 11d (1)), and clean and service the
                                 generator. If reading is still on discharge side, replace voltage regulator. Clean the charging
                                 generator commutator with #000 sandpaper.

                                       Caution: Do not use emery or carborundum cloth on commutator or brushes.
                                      Remove the cover band from the generator. Cut a stick of dry wood the width of the com-
                                   mutator; place a piece of #000 sandpaper over the end of the stick and hold it against the com-
                                   mutator while it is revolving. Blow out all dust, and check the height of the mica between the
                                   commutator bars. If the mica is above the level of the commutator bars or if the commutator is
                                   badly pitted or worn, remove it, and have it turned down in a lathe.
                49 49 Distributor or Magneto. See if the dis g tr o i o b d uto c r ond b i o ti d o y n. and Ex e a xt m e i r n n e al o a t t he t r ach p m ar e t n s ts as a f re oll s o e w c s u : re and in
                                      Distributor block and breaker points (fig. 29). Remove the distributor block by backing out the
                                 screws which hold it in place. Wipe with a clean, dry cloth to remove all traces of dirt. Polish the
                                   brush track with fine crocus cloth (do not use emery or sandpaper). Examine the distributor
                                   brush (2) to see that it moves freely in its holder. Blow out any dirt in the distributor opening
                                   with dry air.

                                    Breaker box and points.  Remove the breaker box cover plate and wipe with a dry cloth to
                                 remove all traces of dust.  Examine the breaker points. Be sure that they meet squarely and

                                 are free of pits and ridges. Use a fine-grit hone to dress the points. Round the stationary point
                                 very slightly to be sure it has no ridge around the edge. Wipe the points with a clean cloth,
                                 making sure that all grease and oil has been removed from this part of the mechanism, If the
                                 breaker points are badly pitted, replace both points. Replace the capacitor as a defective capaci-
                                 tor is frequently the cause of pitting. Apply a small quantity of PET (Pecrolatum) to the fiber
                                 lobe of the moving contact arm, Adjust the breaker points 0.012 to 0.016 inch (0.30 to 0.40 mm)
                                 gap. Use the feeler gage supplied with the unit to check the gap.

50     50  50                         Coil, Wiring, and Switches. All high-voltage wiring (spark plug cables) should be in good con-

                       dition and securely fastened to all support mountings and terminals. All insulation and connec-

                       tions should be secure. If insulation has rubbed or burned off of any cable, it will short circuit.
                       Oil saturated cables and dirty cables may cause the engine to miss. Inspect all low-voltage wiring

                       (safety control circuit) on the engine in the same manner. Inspect capacitors for condition and

                       connections.

                                      Note. Do not tighten wiring connections unless actually loose, as overtightening of terminals will cause damage.

                       Check all the items on the switchboard to see that they are clean, dry, in good condition, securely

                       mounted, and see that all electrical connections are secure. See that all fuses are held securely

                       by their clips, and that contacts are clean.

       51                             Voltage Regulator. Inspect the voltage regulator to be sure that it is securely mounted and in

                       good condition. To test the regulator, start the unit, and, with the unit delivering rated voltage

                       at full load, turn the exciter field rehostat in the resistance-all-in position until the a-c voltage drops

                       about 10 percent. Normal values of a-c voltage can then be controlled by manipulating the
                       exciter field rheostat. To check regulator operation, place the unit on automatic control by

                       turning the exciter field rheostat to resistance-all-out position, and check the voltmeter.

                       voltmeter reading is approximately 240 volts, voltage regulator is operating satisfactorily.

52     52  52          Lights. Check the instrument panel. Be sure that the gree n jewel light is lit during operation

                       of the unit.

53     53  53                         Circuit Breaker. Inspect the circuit breaker to see that it is securely mounted and in good

                       condition.

    57             or       Gages. Check the gages on the engine instrument panel during the operation of the engine and

                            observe as follows:

                                  Lubricating oil pressure. Check oil pressure at frequent intervals. Be sure that the oil-pressure
                               gage is approximately 40 pounds per square inch at governed speed.

                                  Caution: If the gage indicates less than 10 pounds, and the safety switch has not shut the
                              engine down, stop the engine immediately and investigate the cause. (Also check safety switch.)

                                  Water temperature gage. Check the temperature gage to be sure that it indicates in the normal
                              range. The temperature should increase gradually during the warm-up period, and normally
                              should not exceed 170°-190° F. The temperatures at which the gage hesitates during warm-up
                              period indicate the opening of the four thermostats. Extremely low temperatures, after a reason-
                              able warm-up period, may indicate that one (or more) of the thermostats is stuck open. ‘lemper-
                            | ature above normal may indicate that the thermostat is stuck closed, or that the cooling system is
                              clogged. If the gage indicates the water temperature is higher than 205° F., and the safetv switch
                              has not shut the engine down, stop the engine immediately and investigate the cause. (Also check
                              the safety switch.)

    172 172        172 |       Armature, Commutator, and Slip Rings.         Check bearing lubrication and lubricate if due, or
                            if inspection reveals lubrication is necessary.   Remove the exciter wrap-around cover, and inspect
                            the unit. Remove any accumulation of dirt or grease with solvent (SD). Blow dirt out away
                            from the windings with a medium blast of compressed air. Enamine the brushes. Be sure that
                            the pigtails are securely fastened to the brush holders; that exciter brushes move freely in their
                            holders; and that brushes have good contact surface with collector rings and commutator, along
                            the engine brush face. Adjust spring tension according to brush wear. Generator brushes are
                            designed to have a wearing depth of three-quarter inch. The lever assembly and spring are de-
                            signed so that, for each \%-inch of brush wear, correct brush pressure can be maintained
                            by moving the spring arm one notch. If brushes are worn so that correct pressure cannot be main-
                            tained by this adjustment, new brushes must be installed.

                                                              GENERATORS—Continued

173 173 173              Controls, Switchgear, and Wiring. Check wiring for broken insulation and for tight, properly

                         insulated connections. Check the insulation resistance of the windings of the stator and rotor of

                         both the alternator and exciter with a megger (megohmmeter). The insulation resistance of new

                         machines should not measure less than 1 megohm. Although insulation resistance varies greatly

                         in different machines, regulator measurements of the same machine in the same temperature will -

                         reveal any abnormal condition of the insulation, and steps may be taken to remedy it before

                         failure occurs. To determine the insulation resistance of the windings, use the following test

                         procedure:

                         Alternator stator test. Open the circuit breaker; connect the megger to a stator lead. This

                         may be done where either stator lead is connected to the switchboard. Connect the other side

                         of the megger to ground.

                         Alternator rotor test. Lift the brushes from the slip rings; connect the megger between a slip’

                         ring and ground.

                         Exciter armature and field test. Open field switch on control panel. Test by connecting the

                         megger to one of the brushes and ground. Check all items on the switchboard to be sure

                         they are clean, dry, secure, and in good condition, and that electric connections are tight. See

                         that all fuses are held securely by their clips.

174 174 174              Drive Coupling. Remove cover from exciter and coupling, and check for presence and tightness

                         of all nuts, cap screws, and bolts.

(4) Injection pump (fig. 20).

(a) Description. The injection pump is a

constant-stroke, cam-actuated, lapped-

plunger pump. It meters the fuel

accurately, and delivers it at a precise

time, under high pressure, to the

injection spray nozzles, through which

it is injected into the engine cylinders.

The pump, which has a self-contained

drive, consists of an aluminum alloy

housing, with a camshaft compartment

in the lower half, and six pump

assemblies in the upper half. The

camshaft compartment contains a cam-

shaft, turning on ball bearings, sup-

D. Outlet.                                                                              sists of the main filter element, inlet tube, and
                                                                                        prefilter, and the oil cup and disk assembly. Two
        Figure 36. Lubricating oil purifier piping.                                     threaded rods provided with wingnuts hold the
                                                                                        oil cup to the upper assembly. The rods swing
                                                                                        from a band around the body. The lower end
                                                                                        of the rods fit into lugs on the oil cup, and the

                                                                                        wingnuts, screwed onto the ends of the rods, hold
                                                                                        the oil cup in place. Air enters each cleaner
                                                                                        through the inlet tube and strikes the oil-covered
                                                                                        disk surface. This disk deflects the air outward
                                                                                        and upward into the prefilter. The sharp reversal
                                                                                        of air flow creates a centrifugal action which
                                                                                        draws the heavier, dust-laden particles down until
                                                                                        they strike the oil-covered disk. The oil -film,
                                                                                        which is constantly moving outward on the disk,
                                                                                        pushes the heavy dust particles outward and
                                                                                        downward over the disk edges into the oil cup.
                                                                                        Once normal air flow is established, the oil above
                                                                                        the disk is driven outward toward the walls of
                                                                                        the cleaner, and part of this oil is whipped up into
                                                                                        a spray. Particles of foreign matter are picked
                                                                                        up by the globules of oil that constitute this spray
                                                                                        and are thus prevented from being drawn into
                                                                                        the engine. Some of the oil spray is carried with
                                                                                        the air into the outer portions of the prefilter and

                                                     rectifier is used to rectify the single-phase,

                                                     a-c supply to the regulator to a pro-

                                                     portional value of d-c energy. The

                                                     rectified d-c energy is independent of

                                                     frequency changes, and the a-c regulator

                                                     is correspondingly free of frequency error

                                                                 b. VaLvE TESTING.

                                                                    (1) Hold the fuel injection pump lever in the
                                                                           fuel shut-off position.

                                                                    (2) Bar the engine over until the No. 1 piston
                                                                          is coming up on compression. Valve
                                                                          should seat fully and hold a charge so
                                                                           that the pistons bounce back. If there
                                                                           is no bounce-back, it is evidence of
                                                                          compression leakage. Have the engine
                                                                          cranked over while listening for a hissing
                                                                           noise at the air intakes and exhaust
                                                                          outlets. Disconnect the exhaust line
                                                                          and remove the air cleaners to determine
                                                                          the source of the sound. If the hiss
                                                                          appears to come from the intake, it may
                                                                          be assumed that the intake valve in the
                                                                          cylinder under compression at the time is
                                                                          not seating properly. If the hiss appears

    h. Vauve Tapper CLEARANCE. The adjustable

valve tappets are screwed into one end of the

rocker arm. Intake and exhaust rocker arms are

not interchangeable, as intake rocker arms have

no oil groove leading to the valve stem. Intake

running clearance should be 0.025 to 0.027 inch.

Exhaust clearances should be 0.030 to 0.032 inch.

After valves have been ground, add 0.002 inch to

these clearances for the first 10 operating hours.

This must be done to avoid valve burning.

These clearances apply to a cold engine.

    mark on the flywheel (indicating intake

    valve opening on No. 1 cylinder) comes

   a. Removau. To remove the generator sleeve-

the oil ring retaining slip. With oil ring disen-
gaged, tap the bearing lining out. —

which collect dust, dirt, and oil sludge. Draining
the oil flushes the settled material out. When the

114. General                                         e. Other. Use anything immediately availablfeor
                                                  destruction of this equipment.
   The demolition procedures outlined in -para-
graphs 115 and 116 will be used to prevent the       J. Disposal. Bury in slit trenches, fox holes,
enemy from using or salvaging this equipment.     and other holes. Throw in streams. Scatter.
Demolition of the equipment will be accomplished
only upon order of the commander.                 116. Destruction of Components

115. Methods of Destruction                          a. Smash cylinder heads, cylinders, crankcase,
                                                  carburetor, power generator, magneto, all air and
   a. Smash. Use sledges, axes, handaxes, pick-   fuel filters, pumps, radiator, and control panel.
axes, hammers, crowbars, and heavy tools.
                                                     6. Cut all electric wiring, cables, hose, and belts.
b. Cut. Use axes, handaxes, and machetes.            c. Burn fuel, lubricating oil, and technical
                                                  manuals.
   c. Burn. Use gasoline, kerosene,  oil, flame      d. Bend all copper pipes and fuel injection lines.
throwers, and incendiary grenades.                   e. Bury or scatter all smashed pieces after de-
                                                  stroying their usefulness.
d. Kxplosires. Use firearms, grenades, and TNT.     f. Destroy everything.
